金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 10 月 05 日
上週重點訊息
◼ 美國製造業維持擴張，成本壓力升高：9 月 ISM 製造業調查顯示，美國製造業連續第九個月
位於擴張區間，新訂單與就業分項均見改善，反映接單與用人需求仍有支撐。然而，價格指數
由 8 月的 71.1 升至 9 月的 77.9，能源、金屬及關稅相關成本上升，加上供應商交貨偏慢，企
業面臨更大成本壓力 。 後續須觀察企業能否轉嫁成本 ， 以及通膨黏性對聯準會政策判斷的影響 。
◼ 日本短觀顯示製造業改善、服務業略緩：日本銀行 10 月 1 日公布的 9 月短觀顯示，大型製造

業景氣判斷指數由 6 月的＋22 升至＋24，大型非製造業則由＋37 降至＋35。企業預估 12 月
兩項指數分別回落至＋21 與＋30，顯示現況仍有支撐，後續看法則偏審慎。製造業改善有助
支撐日股獲利預期，但服務業放緩及日銀政策路徑仍須持續觀察。
◼ 台灣製造業活動加速擴張，AI 需求續強：10 月 1 日公布的製造業調查顯示，台灣 9 月製造業
活動擴張速度創 2021 年 8 月以來最快，AI 相關應用需求仍是重要推力。國發會 9 月 29 日公
布的 8 月景氣對策信號續呈紅燈，綜合判斷分數為 41 分，與 7 月持平，領先及同時指標皆續
升。科技供應鏈景氣仍有支撐，但成長動能能否擴及其他產業，仍是觀察後續景氣的重點。
市場展望
◼ AI 需求持續擴張，關注財報季表現： AI 基礎建設需求未受利率環境影響，而是持續擴張，帶
動光通訊、CCL、ABF 載板 、記憶體、先進製程與封裝等關鍵供應鏈維持強勁成長，市場表
現由全面上漲轉向結構性分化。隨新一代 AI 伺服器放量、CSP 資本支出維持高檔，具供給優

勢、定價能力及訂單能見度延伸至 2027 年的龍頭廠商仍可望受惠。10 月中旬起陸續公布的
第三季財報，若結果持續表現亮眼，可望為市場再添動能，屆時具 AI 供應鏈題材的台股與日
股亦可望受惠。
◼ 債券佈局聚焦 5~10 年期美國公債及投等債：雖然貨幣政策風險 下降有助於穩定長期通膨預
期，但政府與企業發債供給增加所帶來的排擠效應，以及美國經濟成長展現韌性，仍可能使長
天期利率面臨上行壓力。因此，在當前利率環境下，5~10 年期債券較能兼顧票息收益與存續
期間風險管理。投資級債方面，彭博美國投資級公司債指數利差約 80-90 個基點，雖然利差
偏窄，但高基準利率環境仍提供具吸引力的絕對收益水準，因此現階段5~10 年期美國公債及
投資級債的風險報酬結構相對較佳。

市場焦點
美國
• 9月ISM服務業PMI降至54.9，雖商業活動與新訂單放緩 ，但就業回升至 50.1、訂單積壓增加 ，
顯示服務需求仍具韌性；另一方面，價格指數升至74.0，創2022年7月以來新高，供應商交貨
速度亦放緩 ，反映燃料 、原物料及供應鏈成本升高 ，景氣未明顯轉弱之際 ，通膨壓力反而重
新升溫；同時全球長債殖利率同步走高 ，AI基建投資延續企業成長動能 ，也提高資本需求 。
整體而言 ，美國經濟尚未明顯轉弱 ，但通膨與長端利率壓力同步升高 ，使金融條件維持偏緊 。
中國
• 彭博指出在政府積極推動開源策略與 AI普及下，中國產業快速發展出現供給過剩 ，產業獲利
能力面臨挑戰，未來三年中國AI產業將可能處於虧損狀態，不過即使模型價格下降，AI使用量
增加將帶動Token需求大幅成長，其中具有企業客戶、專有資料及生態優勢的AI龍頭，將比單

純提供模型服務的公司更具長期競爭力。
黃金
• 歐美國家財政可持續性的疑慮持續發酵 ，加上長期通膨風險升溫 ，宏觀不確定性讓實體黃金
市場逆勢吸金 。本波下跌主要反映去槓桿交易 ，實體黃金的基本面未變 ，有望為金價帶來中
期的支撐。建議投資人逢修正分批進場 ，可透過黃金存摺或是實體黃金 ETF(GLD)，以投資組
合多元化的角度布局。波段投機者，從技術指標來看，可留意4,000美元關卡是否出現支撐。

美國9月ISM服務需求略降、成本壓力升高，景氣韌性與通膨風險並存
• 服務業維持擴張，需求略降但就業回穩：9月ISM服務業指數降至54.9(前月55.4)，連續27個月位於擴張區間；商業活動
指數降至56.5、新訂單降至 59.8，顯示服務需求較前月放緩 ，但就業指數回升至 50.1、重返擴張區間 ，訂單積壓升至
56.6，顯示內需仍具韌性，服務業尚未出現明顯轉弱訊號。
• 成本與供應鏈壓力升高 ，通膨降溫面臨干擾： 價格指數升至 74.0，創2022年7月以來新高 ，且為7個月來第6次高於70；
供應商交貨指數升至53.2，顯示交貨速度放緩，燃料、柴油、鋼材、銅及記憶體等成本上升，加上供應受限，使服務業
投入成本壓力擴大，通膨降溫進程面臨干擾。
• 市場觀察：美國服務業為經濟成長的重要支撐，目前經濟呈現「需求仍強、通膨升溫」的拉鋸格局，雖服務業景氣支撐

美國企業獲利，但成本上升可能推升利率波動，投資上宜偏向美國短中天期投資級債，兼顧較高收益率與利率風險控管；
股票則可聚焦具定價能力、獲利與現金流穩健的優質企業。
資料來源：Bloomberg， 2026.10.6 資料來源：ISM Services PMI Report， 2026.10.6
ISM服務業指數分項指標變動對比表ISM服務業PMI主要分項指數變化

全球利率同步走高、AI投資持續擴張，長端美債壓力仍高
• 全球長債殖利率同步走高，反映利率環境重新定價：美國財政部長貝森特指出，近期美債殖利率攀升與德國、日本等主
要債市同步上行，反映全球資本成本重新定價，而非市場對美國資產失去信心；加上目前尚未出現資金大規模撤離美債
並轉向其他主權債券的現象，因此債市波動較偏向全球利率環境調整，而非金融系統性風險。
• AI投資增加資金需求，支撐較高利率環境：貝森特亦駁斥AI泡沫論，認為微軟、Alphabet及Meta等大型科技企業仍以
實際獲利與現金流支撐持續擴大AI資本支出，不同於過去缺乏基本面的科技泡沫；龐大的資料中心 、電力與半導體投資
需求提高整體資金成本，也成為推升長天期利率的重要因素之一。
• 市場觀察：目前市場經濟近況屬於利率高檔、成長尚穩的情境中，因而投資策略建議配置大型科技股、AI基建供應鏈及

受惠利差環境的金融股具相對優勢；債券配置則以中短天期投資級債優先，避免長天期債券受利率上行壓力侵蝕。
資料來源：Bloomberg， 2026.10.6
長端殖利率維持今年以來高檔
資料來源：兆豐銀行整理， 2026.10.6

顧
• 美國8月貿易逆差擴大至2025年初以來最高水平：美國提高進口關稅後，貿易失衡問題仍未明顯改善，商務部數據顯示，8
月貿易逆差擴大13.7%至1,056億美元，且超出市場預期的1,021億美元。經通膨調整後，8月商品貿易逆差擴大至1,147億美
元，同樣為2025年3月以來最大。
• 觀察美國本土投資能否進一步轉化為出口能力：AI建設帶動半導體與設備進口，加上能源成本上升及關稅政策干擾，推升進
口額至歷史新高。不過，強勁內需也顯示消費與企業投資仍具支撐力。隨著第三季財報季即將展開，根據Factset數據，分析
師預期第三季標普500企業獲利年增約29.5%，加上企業獲利預期持續上修，強化投資人對高利率環境下企業基本面的信心。
投資策略上，相較於單筆投資，此時的高成長與高波動的環境，更適合分批布局的定期定額策略來參與市場。

美國進口創下解放日以來的最高水平

顧
• 實質薪資持續成長，內需復甦動能增強：日本8月實質現金收入年增1.5%，連續第8個月成長，名目工資與基本工資均年增
3.8%，顯示薪資成長已逐步抵銷通膨對購買力的侵蝕；剔除獎金、加班費及抽樣偏差後，全職勞工薪資仍成長2.8%，反映
薪資上漲具有一定持續性。實質所得改善有助家庭消費與內需回升，進一步支撐企業營收與獲利展望。
• 薪資與通膨並行，日股中長期仍具配置價值：薪資持續上升有助日本形成薪資增加、進而消費擴大，導向企業營收改善的正
向循環，同時也強化日本央行持續推進貨幣政策正常化的條件。雖然升息可能推升日圓與資金成本，但金融股可受惠於利差
擴大，且企業治理改革、AI投資及自動化需求仍提供獲利支撐。近期日股AI與半導體族群已明顯走強，顯示市場仍重視企業
獲利成長；中長期建議維持日股正向看法，聚焦金融、AI、機器人、自動化及電力設備等具結構性成長題材的企業。

中國AI產業陷入逐底競爭困境
• 中國AI產業陷入逐底競爭困境 ：彭博指出在政府積極推動開源策略與
AI普及下，中國AI產業快速發展 ，但也逐漸出現供給過剩 ，使產業獲
利能力面臨挑戰 。開源模式雖有助於降低技術門檻 、應用普及 ，但也
使技術優勢難以長期維持 ，加上中國市場長期習慣低價甚至免費的數
位服務，多數AI業者難以透過訂閱模式獲利，轉而依賴利潤較低的API
服務作為主要收入來源 。目前中國已備案大型語言模型 (LLM)數量達
988個，包括DeepSeek、Kimi及智譜AI等新創企業快速崛起，進一步
加劇市場競爭。此外API服務逐漸被視為基礎公用服務，即使AI Agent
帶動詞元(Token)需求快速成長，也未必能同步提升獲利，由於開源模
型可由不同雲端業者部署 ，市場進入門檻降低 ，業者更難建立長期競

爭優勢。
• 後市展望： 考量龐大資本支出與持續價格競爭 ，未來三年中國 AI產業
整體仍可能處於虧損狀態 ，市場將形成「美國閉源、高毛利」與「中
國開源 、低成本 」模式 ，即使模型價格下降 ，AI 使用量增加將帶動
Token需求大幅成長 ，進而推升資料中心 、雲端服務及 AI晶片需求 ，
從投資角度來看，基礎設施供應商(晶片、伺服器、資料中心、雲端)受
惠程度可能高於模型開發商；而具有企業客戶 、專有資料及生態優勢
的AI龍頭，將比單純提供模型服務的公司更具長期競爭力。

人行下調抵押補充貸款利率和房貸貼息等政策出台，有望提振中國經濟
和市場信心
• 人行下調抵押補充貸款利率和房貸貼息等政策出台 ，有望提振中國經濟和市場信心 ：日前中國推出購房貸款貼息政策及下調
PSL(抵押補充貸款)利率，代表穩增長政策進一步加碼 ，有助於提振市場信心與經濟預期 ，此次政策不僅支持房地產市場 ，也
將「六張網」建設、科技創新、民營及小微企業 ，納入重點扶持範圍 ，透過增加再貸款額度 ，與提高支持比例 ，帶動投資、
就業及消費回升。短期A股有望在政策推動下出現築底反彈，其中基建投資、製造業投資、房市銷售及居民消費有機會改善，
同時PSL(抵押補充貸款)降息有助於流動性環境邊際寬鬆 ，降低市場對經濟增長的疑慮 ，後市來看企業獲利與信用環境有望逐
步修復，進一步鞏固A股慢牛格局。投資建議上房貸貼息政策有利房地產、建材、家居等產業鏈，金融業受惠資金成本下降與

信貸回升，食品飲料、零售、家電及服務消費則可望受惠於居民消費意願提升。

中國、日本減持美國公債的背後邏輯與差異
• 橋水基金創始人達利歐警告 ，美國公債正面臨中日兩國減持的風險 ，數據顯示這項警告已成事實 。2026年前七個月，中國
美國公債持倉減少660億美元至6,180億美元；同期日本持倉亦減少820億美元至1.1兆美元，扣除價格變動的估值效應後，
兩國實質持倉均大幅下滑。然而，兩國削減美國公債的背後動機並不相同，這將導致未來的減持軌跡出現明顯分化。
• 中國戰略性長期去美元化：1.降低地緣政治風險與轉向資產多元化 ，隨著人民幣在國際貿易結算中使用增加 ，市場流動性提
升，中國對美元儲備依賴度顯著下降。2.美債財政可持續性隱憂，美國政府債務持續攀升，引發對美國長期財政健康疑慮。
3.根據彭博經濟研究模型預測，在漸進式減持情境下，中國的美元儲備(以美債與機構債為主)到2050年可能減少約50%。

• 中國搭配戰術性節奏調整 (短期緩和因素)：儘管長期趨勢向下 ，但目前中美利差顯著 (美中30年期公債利差超過 350個基點)，
在美債殖利率維持高位期間，過快減持意味著實現債券價格下跌的虧損，且可能加劇人民幣升值壓力。因此，中國短中期內
傾向採取「戰略性減持、戰術性放緩」的靈活步調。
美國公債持倉：中國 vs 日本
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.8，美國財政部

日本受政策利率與本土資本回流驅動
• 與中國的結構性考量不同，日本的減持主要出自週期性因素與匯率干預需求。
• 日本央行貨幣政策轉鷹： 日本央行正加快升息腳步 。隨著日債殖利率上揚 (30年期日債殖利率升至約 4.2%)，美日利差持續
收窄，吸引日本國內機構資金回流本土主權債市場。
• 匯率干預政策：日本為防範日圓過度貶值，日本財務省傾向出售部分海外美債資產以籌集外匯干預資金。若日圓再次面臨壓
力，此類拋售干預可能持續。因此，隨著日本央行逐步走向政策正常化，日本對美債的持倉大概會延續下滑軌跡。
• 小結與投資建議：美國公債作為全球無風險資產的地位正面臨結構性挑戰，中國的「長期去美元化」與日本的「本土利率回
升資金回流」將形成雙重壓力，這意味著美債殖利率短期內難大幅下行，美國財政部發債成本居高不下，進而推高全球資本

的融資成本。投資債券配置建議採取「中短天期＋階梯式配置」策略，長天期美國公債(20年以上)受發債供給過剩及中日兩
國長期減持影響，價格波動較大。建議投資人適度降低長天期美國公債暴險，轉向防禦性較佳的中短天期美國公債或高品質
高評級公司債，以鎖定當前較高殖利率同時控管利率風險。
殖利率差：美元與人民幣 vs 美元與日圓
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.29

黃金市場資金流向分歧，實體投資有望支撐中長期金價表現
• 利率與匯率因素，壓抑金價近期表現：9月以來，美國公債殖利率飆升、美元指數走揚，由於黃金本身不孳息，資金傾向轉
往固定收益市場，疊加美元走強壓抑國際購買意願，金價自8月底高點至今已下修約11%。
• 9月資金流向分歧，實體投資強、期貨市場弱：根據世界黃金協會統計，9月在金價下跌之際，全球實體黃金ETF卻吸引大量
資金流入，流入約100億美元(67噸)。另一方面，COMEX黃金期貨市場投機部位大幅下降 ，避險基金與其他機構投資人的
多頭部位明顯減少，造成期貨市場賣壓增加，進一步推動金價下跌。
• 實體黃金市場逆勢吸金反應投資人的避險需求：歐美國家財政可持續性的疑慮持續發酵，加上美伊衝突未解及AI算力需求強
勁，長期通膨風險升溫，宏觀的不確定性讓實體黃金市場逆勢吸金。從數據上來看，本波下跌主要反映去槓桿交易，實體黃

金的基本面未變 ，有望為金價帶來中期的支撐 。建議投資人逢修正分批進場 ，可透過黃金存摺或是實體黃金 ETF(GLD)，以
投資組合多元化的角度布局。波段投機者，從技術指標來看，可留意4,000美元關卡是否出現支撐。
資料來源：世界黃金協會，2026.10.7。

2. 本資料提及之經濟走勢預測不必然代表基金之績效，基金投資風險請詳閱基金公開說明書。
3. 基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益 ，投資人申購
前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 本資料所載內容如涉及商品資訊 ，商品相關資訊仍以經核可之條款及文宣 (DM)為主，本行網站已備有公開說明書可供查閱基金應負擔之費用及相關投資風險 。投
資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。
5. 本資料所載內容取自本公司相信可靠之資料來源，且為特定日期之判斷，本公司不保證其即時性、精確性及完整性，亦不提供或嘗試遊說客戶做為投資之判斷依據。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。
11. 屬基金資訊觀測站ESG基金專區所列之基金，有關基金之ESG資訊，投資人應於申購前詳閱基金公開說明書或投資人須知所載之基金所有特色或目標等資訊。

### Page 1

Flashnote
26 October 2026
PCB / Substrates
TPCA takeaways - PTFE and expanding supply
tightness
What's new
• We attended the TPCA show during Oct 20-22 and hosted meetings with
industry experts. Product readiness/adoption between PTFE and M9-M11
CCL (Copper Clad Laminate) was the most discussed topic. Investors also
tried to find new stock ideas under expanding industry shortages from
production equipment, raw materials, to PCB products.
Why it matters

• PTFE - PCB yield the main bottleneck while alternative solutions catch
up: Nvidia showcasing PTFE (Polytetrafluoroethylene) NVLink board at
OCP (Oct 14) revived the discussion about PTFE CCL used in Nvidia switch
trays. While PTFE posses exceptionally low Dk (latency) and Df (signal loss)
for a CCL material, PCB processability and production yield remain the
biggest hurdles due to its softness and high CTE (Coefficient of Thermal
Expansion) that leads to misregistration, warpage and difficulty of drilling/

lamination. Our checks with some Nvidia PCB partners suggest <10% yield
rate currently. ESG risk is another issue for wide adoption. Meanwhile,
traditional CCL is migrating to M9/10/11, with or without Qglass/glass fibre,
improving electrical performance with relatively better PCB processability.
We believe PTFE is more of a transitional/niche alternative before the
traditional CCL solution further improves.
• Copper foil and substrate's CCL could be the next bottleneck: We

continue to see tightness or shortages across the PCB supply chain, and
end customers are making prepayment/capex sponsorships (for substrates)
or signing LTAs to secure the supply. We expect continued price hikes for
ABF substrates in 2027-28, and likely the same for PCB's CCL, due to supply
tightness and fast migrations. During our exchanges with PCB experts, high-
end copper foil (HVLP4 or above) is getting tighter as adoption increases on
new AI server launches, which could lead to price hike towards end of the

year. And after Tglass shortage eases on Nittobo (3110 JP, NR)'s expansion
in 2027, substrate's CCL could become the next bottleneck. This aligns with
EMC's strategy to expand its substrate's CCL business from 2027.
What now
• We believe EMC's recent correction on PTFE is overdone as the technology
is still far from mature and company also has PTFE-hybrid solutions under
development alongside M9/10 upgrades. Its substrate's CCL business also
looks well-positioned under increasing industry tightness and needs for

diversification. For the CCL supply chain, we also like Doosan as the main
Nvidia compute tray CCL supplier.
• For substrates, we prefer leading players like Unimicron and Ibiden on
higher AI exposure to benefit more from the spec upgrades and industry
shortages.

Doosan (000150 KS) • Delayed CCL capacity ramp-up.
• Higher-than-expected cash outlays for the remaining 29% stake in Siltron.
• Lower valuations of affiliate holdings.
Elite Material (2383 TT) • Potential supply bottlenecks for AI server value chain or a slowdown in AI demand.
• Lower efficiency or higher costs during the ramp-up of new capacity across multiple locations.
• More frequent raw material price increases that the company may not be able to pass through to average selling prices in a timely

manner.
Ibiden (4062 JP) • Delays in capex for AI servers and data centres.
• Slower price increases relative to global peers.
• Technological catch-up by peers in high-end FC-BGA.
• Delays in adoption of new technologies (glass-core substrates and CPO substrates).
• Lower automotive production volumes and weaker demand in the ceramics business.
• A stronger yen, which would be negative.
Kinsus Interconnect (3189 TT) • Slower margin expansion on steeper material price hikes.

• Delayed shipment for new projects (e.g., Rubin) or new ASIC customers.
• T-glass shortages hinder shipment growth.
• Potential share dilution from the private placement (up to 20% dilution) is not factored in our forecasts.
Unimicron Technology (3037 TT) • Tglass supply constraints on ABF substrate growth.
• Slower-than-expected capacity expansion due to execution challenges or longer equipment lead times.
• Increased competition from tier-two substrate makers in the AI server market.

months of the year), (2) new Kunshan capacity (total 1.2mn sheets/month or 21% of the
company’s current capacity) ramp up from December, and (3) the mass volume

For TUC, with an even faster product mix improvement speed (due to the lower base vs.

EMC) and the solid pricing hike uptrend for both high end and mid end CCL (15-20%
QoQ average pricing hike in 4Q), we expect the company to deliver a revenue growth
rate at 18% QoQ, and believe the GM will reach 40.8% (+3.9ppt QoQ) in 4Q26,
despite the slowing demand from key AI ASIC customer due to the transition period of
the ASIC projects whose capacity will be replaced by other high-end Ethernet switch
projects (also M8.5/M9 grade CCL).
Key update - EMC now plan to expand capacity to 15.25mn sheets/month from

5.85mn sheets/month capacity today, with solid demand
EMC’s BOD has approved additional capacity expansion plans extending into
2029/30E. EMC now targets year-end capacity of 8.70/10.45/11.95/15.25mn
sheets/month in 2027/28/29/30E (implying 27% 2026-30E CAGR), which is the first
time EMC announced its new capacity expansion plan after 1H28. EMC mentioned that
capacity expansion is supported by LTAs with customers who secure volumes (EMC does
not proactively initiate LTA negotiations). EMC indicated that despite its proactive

capacity expansion planned for 2027-30E, it would still be insufficient to meet with
customer demand secured under signed LTAs (demand under LTA extends at least 3Y
ahead).
EMC’s near-term capacity expansion remains intact, as the capacity expansion in
Kunshan (with additional 1.2mn sheets/month, 21% of current capacity) could
come online as early as Dec 2026 and continue through May 2027 (with new capacity
coming online every month), the expansion was originally scheduled in 2 phases, with

600k sheets/month each coming online in 1Q27 and 3Q27. The accelerated ramp-up
will offset the rescheduling of Guanyin capacity expansion, where the 300k sheet/month
expansions originally scheduled for 4Q26 have been deferred to 2H27, mainly due to
shortages of construction capacity in Taiwan as well as long equipment lead time +
Taiwan government’s strict policy on labor safety related requirement (however, we only
see delay in Taiwan, while all other plans are basically in-line or earlier than the

company’s original expectation).
Investment view - Solid margin beat + expanding margin, suggesting a stock
performance outlook; buy in the dip for both EMC & TUC
We notice that the BBG consensus for 2027 EMC/TUC GM is only at 38%/34%, and we
see upside risk to consensus EPS estimates for both EMC/TUC as the GM in 4Q could
both surpass 40-42%, without any possibility to see the GM to decrease in coming
quarters as the high-end CCL supply demand gap to further expand in coming years (see

here). As a result, we believe consensus 2027 GM could lift from 34-38% to 40-42%+ for
both companies in coming days, suggesting 10-25% EPS upward revision opportunity,
and recommend taking advantage to buy in this valuation dip (EMC/TUC only trades at
15x/12x of our bullish 2027 EPS estimate, which was far below the past 3 year range of

20-30x).  
Moreover, EMC has mentioned all of its new capacity in coming years (capacity will

2H27, which is only for single customers’ AI GPU projects, but still implies a CCL 
migration trend in-line with our bullish view. For M9, we only see HGX R200 UBB and 
VR200 LPU adopt this solution in 2026, but see multiple new projects in 2027 will adopt 
this high ASP project (including several 1.6T ethernet switch projects, and Trainium 4/ 
TPU v9 could also see at least 50% adoption rate of the M9 CCL; moreover, the VR300 
rack should have 50%+ of its CCL content related to M9 grade CCL). We believe this new 
technology migration trend going into 2027 will further drive both companies’ ASP and 
profit in coming quarters, suggesting an even more favorable outlook for EMC/TUC’s EPS 
growth in coming years.  
Overall, we maintain our bullish view on high-end CCL players’ business outlook and 
margin expansion trend, and expect investors to start focusing on this solid margin 
expansion trend after this clean beat on GM/OPM in 3Q26; maintain Buy on 
EMC/TUC, with a new TP of NT$11,700/NT$3,460 (from NT$10,200/NT$3,010).

3-5+ years. We are positive on its market share trajectory in the server industry and 
expect EMC to increase its share from <10% in the Purley generation to 15-20%/20+% 
in the Whitley/Eagle Stream platforms. EMC is also proactively expanding its share in the 
switch market (40%/30%+ in 2024/23 vs. <5% before 2019), with an expanding AI 
customer base (only one 400G customer in 3Q20, to being one of the largest suppliers in 
2023), which should continue to benefit the company’s top/bottom line growth over 
time. We believe EMC’s leading position in the AI server CCL market should drive strong 
revenue growth and GM/OPM expansion in the long term, given that it is the major 
supplier for all Nvidia/Google/AWS AI server projects, and we expect it to maintain its 
leadership position. Considering its leading position in the high-end CCL industry, solid 
production skills and earnings growth outlook, we are Buy rated on the stock. With the 
shares trading at a 2026E P/E below AI server component suppliers and supported by a 
solid growth outlook, we view valuation as attractive. 
Key downside risks include: (1) RCC to replace all high-end smartphone HDI design; (2) 
rising trade tensions, which could lead to weaker smartphone and server shipments; and 
(3) rising competition from mainland China peers.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Elite Material  
Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$11,700 is based on 27x 2H27-1H28E P/E 
(in line with the peak P/E multiple for AI component suppliers in the past 3 years).  
Key downside risks:  (1) RCC to replace all high-end smartphone HDI design; (2) rising 
trade tensions, which could lead to weaker smartphone and server shipments; and (3) 
rising competition from mainland China peers. 
Investment Thesis - Taiwan Union Technology Corp. 
TUC is a key high-end global CCL supplier that focuses on M7+ grade high-speed CCL 
(key applications include high-end switches (100G+) and AI server materials), with 20%+ 
market share in the past two years. The company continues to expect its unit market 
share in the server industry to increase from 15% in Whitley and Purley processors to 
higher in the Eagle Stream generation, and plans to launch two types of products for 
low-end and high-end customers (T2A and T2C) to gain market share. Also, strong 
growth in 400G/800G switch shipments and new 800G switch shipments, scheduled for 
launch in 2025-27, should continue to be a key demand driver for TUC, in our view. 
Additionally, the company is working on high-end AI projects with CSP and enterprise 
players, which we believe should drive revenue momentum in the long term. We view the 
stock as undervalued vs. Taiwan CCL peers on a P/E basis and rate it Buy.  
Key downside risks include: (1) slower-than-expected share gains in the low-loss CCL 
segment; (2) rising trade tensions, which could lead to weaker server and switch 
shipments globally; and (3) rising competition from mainland China peers.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Taiwan Union Technology Corp. 
Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$3,460 is based on a 22x 2H27-1H28E P/E 
(+2x STDV higher than the past 3-year industry average P/E multiple).

on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

### Page 3

Delta Electronics (2308.TW)
29 October 2026 Citi Research
3
3Q Result Miss, Expecting Recovery in 4Q26
Delta’s 3Q earnings and GM were below expectation due to component shortages
and delays in data center deployment. GM declined 1.6ppts QoQ to 34.1%,
primarily reflecting inventory provisions equivalent to 1.7% of revenue, driven by
supply chain mismatches, customer delivery postponements, and product
specification adjustments. Nevertheless, OPM remained resilient at 16.6%, broadly

flat QoQ and YoY, supported by operating leverage.
Management indicated that AI-related businesses across its four divisions now
account for approximately 50% of consolidated revenue, highlighting Delta’s
increasing exposure to AI infrastructure investment. Management remains
confident in Delta’s competitive positioning, particularly its integrated capabilities
spanning power conversion, power distribution, thermal management, and
infrastructure solutions. Although the industry continues to face component

shortages, especially for certain power semiconductor devices, Delta believes its
supply chain management capabilities compare favorably to competitors. The
company also plans to establish a major manufacturing facility in Penang,
Malaysia, leveraging the region’s established infrastructure supply chain and
engineering talent.
Figure 1. Delta - 3Q26 earnings review
© 2026 Citigroup Inc. No redistribution without Citigroup’s written permission.
Source: Company Reports, Citi Research Estimates, Bloomberg consensus

HVDC Adoption and SOFC Commercialization Create
Additional Long- T erm Growth Opportunities
Delta expects the transition toward high-voltage DC power architecture to
accelerate as AI rack power density continues to increase. The company began
shipping ±400V HVDC solutions in 3Q26, while NVIDIA is promoting 800VDC
architectures for next-generation AI infrastructure. Management believes HVDC
adoption will eventually become widespread given its potential to improve power

conversion efficiency and reduce distribution losses, although standardization,
installation requirements, and shortages of qualified electrical workers could
constrain the near-term rollout. Beyond power conversion, Delta is developing
solid oxide fuel cell (SOFC) solutions to address growing demand for distributed
power generation. The company plans to begin commercial production in 2027,
with an initial manufacturing line providing approximately 100MW of annual

capacity, primarily supporting customer qualification and pilot installations. Initial

customers include domestic utility and industrial users, alongside several overseas
customers. Management emphasized that SOFC shipments will remain limited
initially as the company prioritizes product reliability and qualification.
4Q26 Recovery Expected as Inventory Normalizes, with
Positive Growth Outlook for 2027
Management expects revenue to increase sequentially in 4Q26 as delayed
customer deliveries resume and inventory begins to normalize. Gross margin

should improve from 3Q26 levels, although management remained cautious given
potential year-end inventory adjustments. The 3Q26 inventory provisions largely
reflected customer-specific products rather than structural obsolescence, and
some provisions could potentially be reversed following successful deliveries.
Nevertheless, management acknowledged that inventory buffers will likely remain
structurally higher than historical levels to accommodate volatile AI infrastructure

deployment schedules. Raw material costs have increased approximately 2% year-
to-date, prompting ongoing pricing negotiations with customers, with limited
recovery expected in 4Q26 and a more meaningful impact in 2027. Operating
expenses will continue increasing in absolute terms to support R&D and capacity
expansion. Management remains positive on 2027 revenue growth, although it did
not provide quantitative guidance. Separately, management expects no near-term

shipment disruption from the ongoing Vicor intellectual property litigation.
We lower earnings projection on power shortages, labor constraints, financing
conditions, and regulatory uncertainties could create quarterly shipment volatility,
but we believe its multiyear AI infrastructure growth cycle intact. We maintain Buy
with TP NT$1,715 (based on lower PER of 35x of 2027 EPS, from 40x previously)

### Page 7

Delta Electronics (2308.TW)
29 October 2026 Citi Research
7
Delta Electronics
Company description
Delta was established in 1975 and is the world's leading computer switching
power supply manufacturer. Its business is divided into: 1) Power Electronics
(59% of 2022 revenue), including Switching Power Supply, Components,
Display and EV Solution; 2) Infrastructure (27% of revenue), including Wind
power, Telecom power, UPS, EV Infrastructure, Networking; 3) Automation

(14% of revenue), including Industrial Automation (IA), Building Automation;
and 4) Others ( <1% of revenue).
Investment strategy
We rate Delta Electronics shares as Buy due to its strong execution and the
ability to expand margin through favorable product mix shift to AI power and
thermal solutions. We believe EV business slowdown is in the price, and we
expect margins to improve driven by AI solution spec upgrades and expanding
business scale. Delta is now positioning itself as an infrastructurer enabler

rather than a pure component supplier, which deepens its key position in the
AI supply chain.
Valuation
Our target price for Delta Electronics of NT$1,715 is based on 35x 2027E EPS
estimate. Our target multiple is set at the higher end of its trading range since
2023, which we believe is warranted by Delta’s strong earnings growth and
better product mix prospects. We also believe the company’s leading industry
position, long product development cycle, and strong engagement with

customers should translate into high orders/earnings visibility.
Risks
Key downside risks that could impede the stock from reaching our target price
are: 1) lower contribution from non-PC businesses; 2) products in the new
businesses (LED lighting, electric vehicle, and Voice) growing more slowly
than expected; and 3) PC power supply losing market share.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds

### Page 2

2

耕興為台灣電子產品測試及驗證服務龍頭廠商：耕興成立於 1986 年，以強制性檢
測服務起家 ， 目前為台灣最大民營電子產品測試及驗證服務實驗室 ， 集檢測 、 認證 、
技術服務與零件銷售為一體，提供電子產品 EMC/Safety 等專業認證及防磁零組
件銷售，為全球少數能執行 5G sub-6GHz (FR1)、5G mmWave (FR2)、Wi-Fi 6、
Wi-Fi 6E高速行動通訊 、 無線傳輸產品且第一家取得802.11ay 與 5G NR mmWave
產品檢測認證之公司，並於台灣 /大陸/美國/德國設立實驗室。以營收結構區分，
檢測與認證收入佔營收約 90%、被動元件銷售佔 10%。國際競爭對手包含
UL/BV/PCTEST/SGS/Intertek 等、地區型競爭對手為耀登/東研信超/華測檢測/譜
尼測試等。 
2Q26 財報優於預期：耕興 2Q26 EPS 3.07 元，優於預期的 2.87 元，5G 手機、
AI NB、 企業AP 等產品檢測訂單狀況維持穩定 ， 公司1H26 5G NR市佔率 23.97%、
FCC 高階網通市占率 47.3%，維持領導地位。 
3Q26 FCC 轉單效益開始小幅貢獻：由於 FCC 擬禁止中國實驗室驗證賣往美國的
產品，公司開始收到一些高階測試的轉單，但 FCC 尚未正式全面禁止，故現在貢
獻不多，仍在等FCC 正式決策，預計11 月會有結果，目前粗估若轉單順利會有共
2~6 億元的營收貢獻，整體 3Q26 營運平穩， 預估 3Q26 營收為 12.47 億元
(+0.4%QoQ，+10.7%YoY)，毛利率 46.8%，營業利益率 30.5%，稅後淨利 2.93
億元(-6.3%QoQ，+13.9%YoY)，稅後 EPS 2.88 元。

### Page 3

3

2027 年公司若受益轉單，營運有望小幅創高：FCC 未來擬禁止所有中國實驗室為
在美國使用的電子設備 (手機、PC 等) 進行測試，研究處認為2027 年開始公司有
望受益中國實驗室受限產生的轉單效應，公司在 2027 年已有擴產計畫，地點仍待
公告，若 NTN (非地面網路)需求如預期增加，有相關設備購買需求，明年 CAPEX
有望上看 4 億台幣，整體預期營運可小幅創高，預估 2026 年營收為 49.04 億元
(+9.1%YoY)， 毛利率46.7%， 營業利益率29.3%， 稅後淨利11.39 億元(+3.9%YoY)，
稅後 EPS 11.18 元，2027 年營收為 52.04 億元(+6.1%YoY)，毛利率 47.4%，營
業利益率 31.2%，稅後淨利 12.54 億元(+10.1%YoY)，稅後 EPS 12.31 元，以過
往現金股利配發率 80%來算，預計配發 9.0 元現金股利，換算目前殖利率 4.2%，
維持買進建議，目標價 246 元(20X 2027 EPS)。

陳泳如  
kate401124-71795@esunsec.com.tw

研 究  報 告                
力積電(6770.TT) 
產業：電子–半導體 
記憶體漲價貢獻 4Q26 獲利維持高檔，規劃 3D AI Foundry 新廠  
 投資評價與建議 
3Q26獲利優於預期，且4Q26毛利率有望重回50%以上之高峰，加上明年記憶
體、成熟製程市況展望正向，故我們給予2.3倍PB，持續看好後續營運動能。 
 重點評論分析 
看好4Q26毛利率重回過往50%以上高峰： 基於 ： (1)IPC 、 網通和醫療等應用支
撐力積電DRAM代工需求，Flash業務中，SLC NAND則因記憶體大廠先進製
程產能排擠，獲得車用、網通等小容量應用降規需求挹注；(2) AI伺服器持續
支撐PMIC、分離式元件需求，另考量中介層與Wafer on Wafer於新竹廠區陸
續復線，將有利於3D AI Foundry營收動能回溫。預估有望季增18.3%至252.4
億元，毛利率53.6%，稅後EPS 1.86元。  
正向看待明年展望： 展望明年 ， 我們認為 ： (1)AI需求維持強勁且應用持續擴大 ，
預期記憶體供不應求之情況將至少延續至 2027年，其中市場預估明年DRAM
報價將延續漲勢；(2)邏輯業務中，PMIC、分離式元件等8吋產能持續受惠AI強
勁需求，我們預期明年台系成熟製程廠商產能利用率有望達90%以上，公司規
劃明年漲價約10~20%且產能將以LTA客戶為優先；(3)考量目前無塵室空間幾
乎用罄，公司將開始評估新廠並著重3D AI Foundry產能擴產，但計畫透過設
備租賃、客戶給付預付款形式由客戶參與部分投資金額，以此控制公司資本支
出。我們初步預估明年營收達1,241.8億元，年增60.3%，稅後EPS 10.65元。 
 投資風險 
記憶體報價下滑、3D AI Foundry進展不如預期。

### Page 2

2

| 營運概況  
3Q26獲利優於預期 ：力積電公告3Q26自結營收213.4億元 ， 季增23.4% 、 年增80.3% ， 毛利率47.6% ， 稅後EPS 1.38
元，獲利表現優於預期，因毛利率受惠於平均ASP季增24%、產能利用率由2Q26的87%提升至3Q26的97%。 
4Q26營收雙位數成⻑，看好毛利率重回過往50%以上高峰：展望4Q26，基於：(1)記憶體大廠優先投資HBM並逐
步退出利基型DRAM市場，加上IPC、網通和醫療等應用支撐，使得力積電DRAM代工需求維持穩健，至於與美光
合作之1P製程進展順利，預計2Q27開始試產，Flash業務中，SLC NAND則因記憶體大廠先進製程產能排擠，獲得
車用、網通等小容量應用降規需求挹注，另公司維持MLC NAND今年底tape out之進度；(2)邏輯業務中，儘管消
費性電子需求不佳，但AI伺服器需求強勁持續支撐PMIC、分離式元件需求，其中公司PMIC代工服務已從0.15微米
切入55奈米並開始出貨，且獲客戶三年綁約，反映需求強勁，另考量中介層與Wafer on Wafer於新竹廠區陸續復
線，將有利於4Q26 AI Foundry營收動能回溫。綜上所述，公司估本季營收有望雙位數季增，我們則預估有望季增
18.3%至252.4億元，毛利率53.6%，回到過往50%以上之高峰水準，稅後EPS 1.86元。  
正向看待明年展望：展望明年，我們認為：(1)AI需求維持強勁且應用持續擴大，預期記憶體大廠優先投資先進製程
之策略不變，故認為整體記憶體市場供不應求之情況將至少延續至2027年，其中市場預估明年DRAM報價將延續
漲勢(但漲幅收斂)，隱含公司代工價格具上漲動能；(2)邏輯業務中，半導體庫存維持健康，且PMIC、分離式元件等
8吋產能持續受惠AI強勁需求(其中分離式元件80%產能已簽訂LTA) ， 我們預期明年台系成熟製程廠商產能利用率有
望達90%以上，基於產能吃緊將有利於明年成熟製程廠商議價能力，公司規劃明年漲價約10~20%且產能將以LTA
客戶為優先；(3)3D AI Foundry業務仍為未來投資主軸，且產能規劃與進展大致如期，唯⼀有較新進展之業務則為
Wafer on Wafer規劃1H27量產 ， 另公司考量目前無塵室空間幾乎用罄 ， 公司將開始評估新廠並著重3D AI Foundry
產能擴產，但計畫透過設備租賃、客戶給付預付款形式由客戶參與30~60%的投資金額，以此控制公司資本支出。
我們初步預估明年營收達1,241.8億元，年增60.3%，稅後EPS 10.65元。

Document 200: [000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 실적은 우려를 불식시킬 것SK하이닉스(000660) 실적은 우려를 불식시킬 것실적은 우려를 불식시킬 것 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 실적은 우려를 불식시킬 것SK하이닉스(000660) 실적은 우려를 불식시킬 것실적은 우려를 불식시킬 것 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 9
Status: ok

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
좋은 실적이 상수인 구간 
SK하이닉스의 26년 3분기 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 31.9% 증가한 104조 
6,150억원이다. 이전 전망 대비 증가한 수준이다. DRAM, NAND 관련 데이터는 
이전 전망 대비 물량은 유사했지만 가격 상승폭은 예상보다 높았다. 26 년 3분기 
영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 37.6% 증가한 83.3조원이다. 이전 전망을 상회한 
수준이다. 
실적이 끌고, 수급이 밀고 
지금까지는 높은 실적에 대한 불안보다는 먼 미래에 대한 불확실성을 앞서 주가에 
반영시키는 힘이 더 강하게 작용하다가, 주가가 1,190,000원을 찍은 이후 상승세를 
보이고 있다. 실적을 발표한 10월 28일도 10% 넘게 상승했다. 기대 이상의 실적과 
미국 반도체 시장 분위기가 좋았던 영향이다. 앞으로는 악재에 대해서 민감도가 떨
어지는 구간에 진입할 것으로 전망한다. 지나치게 하락한 주가와 밸류에이션이 버팀
목이 될 것이고, Anthropic의 IPO까지 발표되는 Big Tech 기업들의 실적은 시장의 
우려를 충분히 상쇄할 수 있을 것으로 기대한다. 추가로 진행되는 자사주 매수도 불
안한 수급을 해소할 수 있을 전망이다. 
목표주가는 괴리율을 고려해서 하향 조정 
SK하이닉스에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. 26년에도 AI 서버 중심으로 메모리 
시장은 성장할 것으로 기대하고, 실적 대비 주가는 저평가 국면에 있다고 판단하기 
때문이다. 26년 급격히 하락한 주가는 업황, 펀더멘탈을 설명하기에는 과한 움직임이라 
판단한다. 목표주가는 4,000,000원에서 3,200,000원으로 하향한다. 메모리 시장의 
업황에 대한 우려가 아니라 현재 주가와의 괴리율을 고려한 수준이다. 27년 EPS 
538,187원에 PER 6배를 적용하였다.

26년 3분기, 모든 악재를 잠재운 실적 
SK하이닉스의 26년 3분기 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 31.9% 증가한 104조 
6,150억원이다.  이전 전망 대비 증가한 수준이다. DRAM, NAND 관련 데이터는 이전 
전망 대비 물량은 유사했지만 가격 상승폭은 예상보다 높았다. 
DRAM 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 35.4% 증가했다. B/G 는 +11.5%, ASP 는 
+28.2%로 추정했다. 3분기 DRAM 가격 상승률은 경쟁사 대비 높은 수준이다. Server 
DRAM 비중이 2분기 대비 높아진 것과 고객 믹스가 개선된 영향으로 추정한다. 
HBM4 비중은 30%를 상회한 것으로 추정한다. HBM이 3분기 가격에 미친 영향은 
제한적인 수준으로 판단한다.  
NAND 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 18.2% 증가했다. B/G 는 -1%, ASP는 +26%로 
추정한다. 이전 전망치를 상회했다. SSD 비중은 2분기 75%에서 3분기에는 77%로 
상승했다.  
26년 3분기 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 37.6% 증가한 83.3조원이다. 이전 전망을 
상회한 수준이다. DRAM 영업이익은 지난 분기 대비 42.3% 증가한 65.1조원으로 
예상한다. 영업이익률은 전 분기 대비 4.0%p 상승한 83.0%로 추정한다. NAND 
영업이익은 전 분기 대비 23.1% 증가한 18.2조원으로 추정한다. 영업이익률은 전 
분기 대비 2.9%p 높아진 72.4%로 추정한다.

26년 4분기, DRAM 물량 효과 
SK하이닉스의 26년 4분기 매출액은 26년 3분기 대비 29.4% 증가한 135.3조원으로 
예상한다. 가격상승은 지속될 것으로 예상하지만 26년 3분기 대비 상승폭이 낮아질 
전망이다. 물량은 3분기 대비 25% 증가할 것으로 전망한다. 신규 라인 가동 영향이다. 
4분기 영업이익은 26년 3분기 대비 34.1% 증가한 111.7조원으로 예상한다. DRAM 
90.9조원, NAND 20.7조원이다.  
DRAM 매출액은 26년 3분기 대비 36.4% 증가한 107조원으로 예상한다. ASP 는 
+15%, Bit Growth는 +25.0%로 가정하였다. HBM 출하는 지난 분기 대비 증가할 
것으로 예상하고, HBM4 비중은 60%대로 높아질 전망이다. 
26년 4분기 NAND 매출액은 26년 3분기 대비 11.6% 증가할 것으로 예상한다. Bit 
Growth는 +5%로 전망하고, ASP는 +12%로 예상한다. SSD 비중은 소폭 개선될 
전망이다. SSD 내에서 eSSD 비중은 90% 수준이다.

IBKS RESEARCH │5 
공정 전환 속도는 예상보다 빠르게 진행 중: 1cb nm, 321L 
SK하이닉스는 1c nm 비중을 빠르게 확대하고 있다. Wafer 투입이 제한된 상황에서 
공급 확대를 위한 최선의 방법이기 때문이다. HBM 수요는 1b nm를 중심으로 대응할 
예정이다. 이와 관련된 물량도 꾸준히 증가하지만, 출하 Bit 기준으로 1c nm 비중이 
26년 1분기 3% 수준에서 26년 3분기에는 20%, 연말에는 33%까지 높아질 전망이다. 
HBM이 아닌 DRAM 물량이 크게 증가할 전망이다. 이를 통해서 높아진 DRAM 수요 
대응할 것으로 예상한다. 26년 출하 Bit Growth는 28.5%로 상향되었다. 
NAND 생산라인의 공정 전환은 High-End로 꾸준히 변화 중이다. 321L 는 26년 
2분기에는 176L와 Cross over 되었다. 예상보다 1분기 빠른 속도다. 26년 3분기 321L 
비중은 50%이다. 26년말까지 321L를 64%까지 확대할 계획이다.

6 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
Check Point 
1. DRAM ASP의 차별화 
26년 3분기 DRAM ASP는 메이커별로 차별화가 크다. 26년 2분기 대비 상승폭은 마
이크론이 10% 후반, 삼성전자는 20% 초반으로 추정하고, SK하이닉스는 20% 후반이
다. LTA, HBM 등 다양한 변수가 있지만 SK하이닉스는 고객 비중 변화가 가장 큰 변
수라고 설명한다.  
2. HBM4 물량, 수율 문제 
26년 3분기에 가장 시끄러웠던 변수가 SK하이닉스의 HBM4 관련 품질 문제이다. 26
년 3분기 DRAM 매출과 영업이익으로는 문제가 있었다고 판단하기 어렵다. 26년 3분
기 HBM에서 HBM4 비중은 30%를 상회한 것으로 추정되고 4분기 말에는 비중이 60%
까지 높아질 것으로 전망한다.   
3. HBM 8Hi 확대가 시장 규모 감소는 아니다. 
HBM4는 12Hi가 원래 제품 사양이다. HBM 공급이 부족해지면서 GPU를 좀 더 많이 
팔기 위해서 적용되는 HBM 용량을 줄이는 방향으로 제품 설계가 변경되는 중이다. 
2027년 하반기에는 8Hi 비중이 12Hi 비중을 상회할 것으로 예상한다. 하지만 이로 
인해서 HBM 전체 물량이 감소하는 것은 아닌 것으로 전망하고 있다.  
4. 투자와 생산은 다르다 
SK하이닉스는 용인팹을 공기를 앞당겨서 완공할 계획이다. 이로 인해서 공급량이 예
상 대비 커지는 것에 대한 우려가 있지만 건물 완공과 장비 반입에는 시차가 있고, 수
요를 고려하지 않은 장비 반입은 없을 것으로 밝혔다. 
5. 주주환원. 더 남아 있다 
공시를 통해서 26년 회계연도 기준 약 100조원의 주주환원 계획을 밝혔다. 배당을 30
조원 이상, 자사주 매입/소각을 60조원이상을 추진할 계획이다. 26년 4분기 중에 배
당은 14조원을 집행하고, 자사주는 20조원을 매입할 예정이다. 자사주 20조원 중에서 
10조원은 종업원 상여금 지급용이어서 주주환원에 포함되지 않는다.  
26년 4분기 실적 발표에서 추가적인 배당과 자사주 매입에 대한 계획을 발표할 것으
로 예상한다.

ASP 움직임은 메이커별로

IBKS RESEARCH │7 
펀더멘탈과 센티멘탈의 줄다리기 구간 
SK하이닉스에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. AI 서버 중심으로 메모리 시장은 
성장할 것으로 기대하고, 실적 대비 주가는 저평가 국면에 있다고 판단하기 때문이다. 
26년 급격히 하락한 주가는 업황, 펀더멘탈을 설명하기에는 과한 움직임이라 판단한다.  
목표주가는 4,000,000원에서 3,200,000원으로 하향한다. 메모리 시장의 업황에 대한 
우려가 아니라 현재 주가와의 괴리율을 고려한 수준이다. 2027년 EPS 538,187원에 PER 
6배를 적용하였다.  
아직 제대로 보급되지 않은 Agent AI가 확산되면 컴퓨팅 파워 필요성에 대한 인식도 
크게 바뀔 것으로 전망한다. 메타의 MUSE는 가입자당 8GB의 DRAM을 가정하고 
있다. Open AI, Google, Anthropic 에서도 Agent Platform이 공개된다면 필요한 
DRAM은 폭발적으로 증가할 것으로 전망한다. 
메모리 수요에 대한 규모가 너무 커지는 것이 걱정이지 누가 살 것인지에 대한 것은 
걱정할 문제가 아니라 판단한다. 기존 AI 모델의 진화, 효율적 모델의 등장, Agent AI 
모델의 확대 모두 메모리 수요를 기하급수적으로 확장시키는 변수이다. 
투자 주체들의 자금난이 수요를 저해하는 변수는 아닌 것으로 판단한다. 대체 
투자자들은 지속적으로 증가할 것으로 전망하기 때문이다.  
메모리 업체들의 투자 계획은 수요에 대응하기 위한 투자일 뿐 공급과잉을 만들 
수준은 아닐 것으로 판단한다.

그림 5. SK하이닉스 투자자별 순매수 추이(2010년부터 누계)  그림 6. SK하이닉스 마이크론 밸류에이션 비교

자료: 에프앤가이드 QuantiWise, IBK투자증권  자료: Bloomberg, IBK투자증권

동 자료에 언급된 종목의 지분율 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
• Central banks’ communication is carefully scrutinised for
every nuance. Lately, there seems to have been more RBA
communication to pore over. The method of delivery has
also evolved. This has sometimes caused confusion when
tone seems to vary. Different audiences call for different

tone and varying complexity.
• The content has evolved, too. RBA officials are increasingly
using speeches directed to professional audiences to
discuss more technical estimates of inferred variables such
as neutral rates and financial conditions. They are also
highlighting that monetary policy combats inflation in more
ways than just squeezing mortgage holders.
• One thing has not changed: the RBA is not in the business
of providing ‘forward guidance’ promises about the future

path of interest rates. It does, however, give more running
commentary about how it is interpreting recent data. These
interpretations are themselves useful signals about what
the RBA thinks it might do.
Market participants pore over every utterance by central banks
for clues to future rate moves. They will have plenty to pore over
in the run-up to the November RBA decision. After the flurry
of fireside chats this week, ’60 Minutes’ will run a pre-recorded

interview with chief economist Sarah Hunter on Sunday. Next
Wednesday, the whole senior policy team will appear before
Senate Estimates, just as the quarterly CPI is released. (This
timing will have been the Senate’s choice, not the RBA’s.) After
the decision will come the Statement on Monetary Policy, a
media release and press conference. More speeches will follow,
including one by the Deputy Governor on the Thursday.
RBA communication does seem to have changed, not just

following the RBA Review but also more recently. There may
be fewer policy meetings each year, but the communication
outside that certainly feels more frequent than a couple of years
ago. Back in 2024, speaking engagements felt a little sparse
outside the post-meeting media conference and parliamentary
appearances. We have counted 58 public engagements so
far this year on the Speeches page on the RBA’s website.
This compares with 58 for the whole of last year and 52 in

2024. Excluding the ‘compulsory’ press conferences and
parliamentary appearances, fewer than half of the 2024 events
were related to economics, markets and monetary policy.
The ‘how’ has changed
The way the RBA communicates with markets has changed
as well. There are fewer set-piece speeches on policy and a
Open mouth operations
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
lot more semi-scripted ‘fireside chats’. Compared with a few
years ago, there are more interviews across a wide range of

media. This is a big change from the pre-Review world. It was
a huge deal when the previous Governor appeared on the
7:30 Report ; it is hard to imagine him agreeing to an Assistant
Governor appearing on 60 Minutes.
Part of the change reflects increased efforts to reach a general
audience. Central banks want to explain why they do what
they do. This matters especially when interest rates are rising
but the household sector is not the source of the inflation.

Speaking differently to different audiences can sometimes
confuse markets. As the RBA’s Chief Communication Officer
highlighted in a recent panel discussion, the post-meeting
media conference is designed to speak to the public. Market
participants and economists certainly tune in, but we are not
the primary audience. So, if you think that what the Governor
said in the media conference sounded more ‘dovish’ than
what another official says in a more market-oriented forum,

it is partly because she is trying to convey that the RBA
understands that its recent policy actions are making things
harder for many Australians. But this is in much the same way
that a doctor might say, “This might hurt a bit.” It does, but that
will not stop the treatment.
A related issue is that it is easier to control tone in pre-drafted
material such as the minutes and set-piece speeches than in
unstructured Q&A. Senior central bankers are trained to be

careful with their words in unscripted settings. If the tone
differs, the minutes are still more likely to say what the Board
really means than an off-the-cuff answer.
Greater communication with the broader public might have
reduced the pressure to make the set-piece speeches more
accessible. Research on central bank communication often
uses measures such as the Flesch–Kincaid Grade Level and
Flesch Reading Ease score. A comprehensive analysis was

beyond the scope of this note. However, a spot comparison
suggests four speeches from 2026 ( 1, 2, 3, 4) were harder
to read than four from 2021 (1, 2, 3, 4). The 2021 speeches
scored in the 11 to 12 range, which is high-school-level
“The RBA does not want to promise what it will
do, but it is increasingly trying to tell us what
it thinks. "

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
complexity. The 2026 speeches scored between 12 and
15, which is academic or technically dense text requiring
university-level education. (Including two of my own 2021
speeches is an obvious caveat, but the two speeches by then-
Governor Phil Lowe also scored better on these metrics than

the sample from this year. It should be noted, though, that
readability metrics do not necessarily imply better clarity if the
concepts discussed are more abstract.)
The ‘what’ has changed
As noted above, the content of RBA communication has
evolved, too. As its intellectual framework has begun to put
more weight on the balance of supply and demand, and those
invisible ‘star’ variables (NAIRUs, neutral real rates, ‘financial
conditions’, etc.), the communication to professional audiences

has increasingly focused on those topics.
More recently, the RBA has also placed more emphasis on
the full range of transmission channels from interest rates to
inflation (though not, as we have noted previously, the labour
supply channel). This is understandable in a world of supply
shocks. People need to understand that monetary policy is not
simply an exercise in hitting mortgage borrowers. We doubt
they would be comforted, though, by the Deputy Governor’s

explanation this week that the ‘cashflow channel’ was the
smallest of the transmission channels from interest rates
to inflation, with the exchange rate much more prominent.
That implies a lot of pain for a small group, for little effect on
inflation. It raises the question of whether other policy tools
or institutional arrangements might get the same effect on
inflation through the other channels without hitting a minority
of households disproportionately.

We have also seen a move away from using catchphrases
to summarise the position. From ‘narrow path’ to ‘not
ruling anything in or out’ to ‘cautious and predictable’, once
entrenched, a phrase can outlast its usefulness.
One thing that has not changed is the way the minutes record
the presentations to the Board and the members’ discussion. The
minutes rarely convey much new information beyond the post-
meeting statements. Sometimes, though, the minutes reveal the

Board’s reaction to the staff’s analysis. The August 2024 minutes
were a good example: the discussion highlighted just how
sensitive the forecasts were to key judgements by the staff.
The ‘why’ has not changed
Despite these changes, the underlying objectives of the RBA’s
communication have not changed. It wants to explain – and
perhaps justify – its decisions. And it wants to describe how
it interprets incoming data, especially inflation, the labour

market and the balance of supply and demand.
The RBA, like any central bank, is not really in the business
of providing ‘forward guidance’ about future moves.
Circumstances change, so it can never guarantee a particular
future path for interest rates. Some academic literature argues
that promising to keep rates low for a long time (or high,
for that matter) independently amplifies the transmission
of monetary policy beyond the effect of current-period

interest rates alone, by shifting the short end of the yield
curve. Central banks including the RBA have certainly tried
jawboning in this way from time to time. The prevailing view,
though, is that the potential cost of reputational loss if things
do not turn out as predicted outweighs the benefits of a
slightly stronger transmission mechanism.
In this context, it is the interpretation of data that provides
the signal about future policy directions. This is why it was

noteworthy that the Governor still characterised the labour
market as ‘a little tight’ after the September meeting, even
though the Board’s post-meeting communication did not. We
have been noting for some time that the RBA staff have a
downbeat view of supply capacity. Together with the RBA’s
oft-stated concerns about inflation, this is why the Deputy
Governor’s ‘hawkishness’ did not surprise us.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: managing expectations
While annual retail sales growth improved in September,
rising from 0.4%yr to 1.1%yr, the current pace of growth
is only a quarter of the gain seen just a year ago and very
weak versus history. Consumer spending continues to be

constrained by weak confidence, particularly with respect to
employment prospects and wealth. On housing wealth, five
years into the property downturn, house prices continue to
decline month-on-month and investment remains in a deep
downturn, –20.6%ytd.
Total fixed asset investment has been detracting from
annual GDP growth since April owing to a lull in high-tech
investment, a lack of confidence in future demand amongst
domestically oriented businesses as well as property’s

malaise. For aggregate growth to hold up through 2027 and
2028 as the contribution from net exports moderates, broad-
based stimulus in scale will prove necessary. Expect recent
targeted support measures to be broadened and given much
greater scale in coming months.
Finally, in the UK, inflation accelerated from 3.1%yr to 3.5%yr
in September, exceeding both market expectations and the
Bank of England's July forecast. The surprise was largely
driven by energy, fuel prices rising 5.4%mth. However, core

inflation also edged up from 2.6%yr to 2.8%yr as services
inflation increased from 3.4%yr to 3.7%yr. While not an
immediate concern inflation, the labour market showing
resilience, the ILO unemployment rate falling from 4.9% to
4.7% in August, suggests demand risks for inflation will need
close monitoring as well as supply-side concerns.
Next week will see the focus once again return to the US as
the FOMC meet and US Q3 GDP is released. For both events,

the implications for risks to the outlook will be the focus. The
week after, the US mid-term elections are due.
With little domestic data of significance, participants’
attention focused on RBA Deputy Governor Hauser's fireside
chat, which saw an abrupt shift in market expectations of a
follow-up rate hike at the November meeting.
Hauser warned in his remarks that the current inflation outlook
was "materially worse" than the RBA's August forecasts.

Three upside inflation risks were characterised as having
"crystallised" ahead of the September meeting – the Middle
East, the AI boom, and domestic supply constraints. Hauser
noted the Middle East picture "looks worse" given oil prices
are "about a third higher than [the RBA] assumed in August”.
He then positioned AI and data centre developments among
an "extraordinary sequence of positive demand shocks around
the world", with "higher AI prices, tech prices feeding through

into CPI". Finally, on domestic capacity pressures, the Deputy
Governor noted that "inflation's been coming in at or a little
bit above expectations, despite an apparent easing in the
labour market". He went on to state that the staff are currently
assessing "whether [the RBA] need to change [its] judgement
on capacity pressures in the labour market again" – in other
words, revise up their assumption for the NAIRU.
Hauser's comments prompted a material reassessment of

market pricing for a follow-up November hike, from just a
20% chance at the end of last week to a 50/50 proposition
today ahead of next week's Q3 CPI. For that release, we are
forecasting a 1.3% (4.1%yr) lift in headline inflation and a
0.9 (3.5%yr) increase in trimmed mean inflation. Our preview
provides more detail behind the component mix for Q3 as
well as our upward revisions to the preliminary Q4 forecast.
Provided inflation prints broadly in line with our expectations,

the RBA is likely to hike by 25bp at the November meeting.
Across in New Zealand, consumer prices rose 0.7% in Q3
and annual inflation eased from 4.1%yr to 3.7%yr. Under
the surface of this ‘as expected’ read though, several closely
watched measures of core inflation took a step higher to now
be at or slightly above the top of the RBNZ’s target band. Our
NZ economics team consequently expect the RBNZ will raise
the OCR 25bps to 3.00% at its October policy review, while

retaining a 4.00% terminal OCR forecast for September 2027.
Earlier in the week, China's economy surprised  to the upside in
Q3, GDP growth accelerating from 0.9%qtr to 1.2%qtr, lifting
annual growth from 4.3%yr to 4.5%yr – keeping authorities on
track to achieve their 4.5–5.0% full-year growth objective.
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
RBNZ now expected to hike in October
We now expect the RBNZ will deliver a 25bp hike at its
policy review on 28 October. While September quarter
inflation was slightly below the RBNZ’s forecast, the
underlying detail was firm. More recently, increases in

fuel prices point to a material lift in inflation by year-end.
We expect further interest rate increases over the coming
months, with the OCR reaching a peak of 4% next year.
Softer headline inflation but firm core pressures
leave RBNZ on course to hike in October
We now expect the RBNZ to raise the Official Cash Rate
by 25bp at its October policy review (previously, we had
expected a pause at this meeting). This change in view reflects
significant upside risks to near-term inflation relative to the

RBNZ’s previous forecast.
While in line with our forecast, the September quarter inflation
figures were slightly softer than the RBNZ expected, with prices
rising 3.7% over the past year (compared with the RBNZ’s
forecast of 3.9%). However, the details were less reassuring.
Excluding volatile items such as food and fuel, underlying
inflation pressures remained firm. Closely watched measures
of core inflation moved higher and now sit close to, or a little

above, the top of the RBNZ’s target band. We also saw an
increase in the number of goods recording price rises, taking
that measure back to levels seen in the wake of the pandemic.
Alongside the existing firmness in underlying inflation, the
risks for inflation over the coming quarters are firmly tilted to
the upside. That near-term upside risk has also been noted by
the Governor. Following a sharp rise in global fuel costs, we
expect inflation to reach 4.3% by the end of this year (above

the RBNZ’s September MPS forecast for +3.9%). While that is
mainly due to higher prices at the pump, businesses in sectors
such as construction are also reporting related increases in
transport and materials costs. Consistent with those trends,
we expect core inflation to remain in the upper part of the
RBNZ’s target band through 2027.
Reinforcing the case for an OCR hike at the October meeting,
the New Zealand dollar is notably weaker than the RBNZ

assumed: on a trade-weighted basis, the NZD is currently
3.6% below the RBNZ’s September projections. If sustained,
that would add to inflation pressures over the next few
quarters. We suspect this will not have shifted the RBNZ’s
medium-term outlook for inflation.
Several other developments need to be weighed against that
firmer inflation outlook. First, wholesale long-term interest rates
have risen markedly, and there has also been a more moderate

rise in some mortgage rates. The RBNZ is likely to note the role of
those increases in managing medium-term inflation risks.
In addition, the RBNZ is likely to continue to emphasise that
there is little evidence of a lift in businesses’ longer-term plans
for price setting at this stage in response to the rise in energy
prices (sometimes referred to as “second-round inflation
effects”). We expect the RBNZ to highlight the risk that more
persistent inflation pressures could emerge, depending on the

duration of the oil price shock.
We also expect the RBNZ to note that the labour market
remains cyclically weak and is not a significant driver of inflation.
Employment growth that remains weak relative to labour force
growth, subdued growth in job vacancies and low labour market
turnover are consistent with this view and could be emphasised.
Finally, the RBNZ is likely to acknowledge that while the
global growth outlook remains solid, there are significant

downside risks from the Iran war and concerns about the
sustainability of the AI investment cycle.
Balancing those concerns, we think the MPC will lift the
cash rate next week. However, a vote seems very possible as
differences in view will likely remain among the MPC about
how much the medium-term inflation outlook has really
shifted in recent weeks.
In September, the bulk of the MPC saw upside risks to the
inflation outlook while two members (Conway and Hansen)

thought the risks were balanced. Recent developments will
not have made those seeing upside risks to inflation more
Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
comfortable, while the more dovish MPC members will
likely focus on the ongoing downside risks for growth given
continued softness in house prices and the mounting pressure
on real incomes. Indeed, all MPC members saw some degree
of downside risks to growth which likely remain relevant. All

these messages should remain in the RBNZ’s updated views.
The MPC is likely to continue to present its decision as a
gradual evolution of the approach taken since July: a higher
OCR now provides insurance against the need for a much
higher OCR later if upside risks to medium-term inflation
materialise. We doubt the aim will be to push market interest
rates materially higher.
RBNZ to leave door open for further hikes, OCR
still expected to peak at 4% in 2027

In its forward guidance, the RBNZ is likely to leave open the option
of raising the OCR further at the December Monetary Policy
Statement, without presenting an increase as certain. While the
RBNZ will not publish an updated OCR projection at this review,
the press statement is likely to note that further increases may
be needed, depending on economic conditions. The September
projection showed the OCR eventually rising to 3.25%.
We expect the RBNZ will end up delivering another 25bp

hike in December, followed by a further hike in February.
That would take the OCR to 3.5%, roughly where the RBNZ
estimates the neutral rate to be. At that point, we expect the
RBNZ to slow the pace of rate increases (previously, we had
expected that point to be reached a little later).
We continue to expect further 25bp hikes at the June and
September 2027 meetings, taking the OCR to a peak of 4.0%.
While oil prices are generating significant swings in inflation,

underlying pressures remain firm. More restrictive conditions are
likely to be needed to bring core inflation sustainably back to 2%.
Scenarios
Our central scenario, with a probability of around 60%, is that
the RBNZ increases the OCR by 25bp and signals that a further
increase in December remains an option, without committing to
a markedly tighter future stance than it outlined in September.
Alternative scenarios include:
• A hawkish scenario (around a 20% probability) would see

the OCR increase to 3%, with the RBNZ placing greater
emphasis on upside risks to core and medium-term inflation.
That would suggest an upward revision to the RBNZ’s
projected OCR path in December. Downside risks to the
exchange rate could also be emphasised.
• A dovish scenario (around a 20% probability) would
see the RBNZ leave the OCR unchanged and signal that
the medium-term inflation outlook has not changed
meaningfully from its September forecast, despite higher

near-term inflation. In this scenario, the RBNZ would
reiterate its previous forecast: a 25bp increase in December
and one further hike during 2027.
How might the election play into the decision?
While next week’s meeting is close to the General Election
on 7 November, the decision taken by the MPC will not be
politically motivated. However, the election outcome is close.
And should it result in a Labour Party-led coalition, it seems
likely the new government would adopt the Labour Party’s

promise to restore the RBNZ’s dual mandate. That would
require the MPC to weigh the degree of labour market slack
more highly in its deliberations. As the degree of labour market
slack currently seems high, a new mandate might justify the
MPC taking more time to return inflation to the middle of the
target range (our earlier note discusses this issue). This could
figure into the deliberations of some MPC members if the
decision between leaving the OCR unchanged and raising it

now is viewed as finely balanced.

AUS: Sep Private Sector Credit (%mth)

Private sector credit growth came in slightly firmer than
expected at 0.6%mth in August, above Westpac’s and the
market’s forecast for a 0.5%mth gain, leaving credit growth

running at 8.4%yr. Growth in housing credit continued to ease,

NZ: RBNZ Official Cash Rate Review (%)
Oct 28, Last: 2.75, Westpac f/c: 3.00, Mkt f/c: 3.00
We expect the RBNZ to raise the OCR by 25bps to 3% at
the 28 October Review. Data since September show a higher
outlook for year-end inflation and a stronger GDP starting

point, but increased risks to domestic consumption and
residential investment. On balance, the medium-term inflation

NZ: Oct ANZ Business Confidence (index)
Oct 29, Last: 51.9
Firms remained optimistic in the September survey of business
confidence, with both the activity and inflation measures
broadly holding steady. However, it’s likely that most responses
were submitted in the early part of the month, while fuel prices
took a sharp step higher from the second half of September. The

survey comes too late for the RBNZ’s October policy review, but
evidence about the breadth of cost and price pressures could
have a bearing on the RBNZ’s thinking about the total amount of
policy tightening needed in the months ahead.
NZ: Sep Monthly Employment Indicator (%mth)
Oct 28, Last: 0.2, Westpac f/c: 0.3
The MEI has been gradually ticking higher in recent months,
showing that the jobs market has held its ground through
the Iran conflict and the consequent surge in fuel prices. The

weekly snapshots provided by Stats NZ have been running
ahead of year-ago levels and suggest another solid result for
September – we’ve pencilled in a 0.3% increase. Given the
history of this measure, however, both this month and the
previous month are likely to be revised down a little from their
initial release.

Oct FOMC meeting
Oct 27-28, Last: 3.875%, Mkt f/c: 3.875%, WBC f/c: 3.875%
Core CPI inflation and producer price inflation both surprised
to the upside in September, and the price of Brent oil has
risen back to around USD110. Underlying momentum in
the economy also remains robust, although employment

disappointed in the latest update. Given the concerns shown
by Committee members over price risks and their collective
resolve to return annual inflation to target, the above data
could easily justify a follow-up hike at the October meeting.
That said, Committee members who have spoken recently
have shown patience with respect to assessing the economy
and positioing policy. The market has responded, effectively
pricing out the chance of an October hike in favour of a

December move, which is also our expectation.
US Q3 GDP
Oct 29, %ann’d, Last: 2.2%, Mkt f/c: 3.0%, WBC f/c: 3.1%
The US economy continues to grow at a pace well above trend.
This outperformance is almost entirely due to the strength of
investment in data centres and other AI related assets. The
pipeline for this activity remains strong, limiting near-term
risks to the downside.
Abstracting from AI investment’s contribution, other business
investment and residential investment is weak, weighed down

by high term interest rates and uncertainty related to trade
policy. Household consumption growth is volatile month-to-
month, but the trend is solid. Absent a marked deterioration
in the labour market, which is not our expectation, this
momentum is likely to persist, as wealth continues to grow and
household income gains neutralises inflation. Come 2027 and
2028, growth is expected to slow to trend, but not below.

Document 369: Open mouth operations (2026-11-01)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Open mouth operations (2026-11-01).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 12
Status: ok

### Page 1

#australia #luciellis #rba
Open mouth operations
By Luci Ellis
Chief Economist Westpac Group, Westpac
13:34 October 23 2026 Share
The RBA does not want to promise what it will do, but it is increasingly trying to tell us what
it thinks.
Central banks’ communication is carefully scrutinised for every nuance. Lately, there seems to have
been more RBA communication to pore over. The method of delivery has also evolved. This has

### Page 2

Market participants pore over every utterance by central banks for clues to future rate moves. They will
have plenty to pore over in the run-up to the November RBA decision. After the flurry of fireside chats
this week, ’60 Minutes’ will run a pre-recorded interview with chief economist Sarah Hunter on Sunday.
Next Wednesday, the whole senior policy team will appear before Senate Estimates, just as the
quarterly CPI is released. (This timing will have been the Senate’s choice, not the RBA’s.) After the

decision will come the Statement on Monetary Policy, a media release and press conference. More
speeches will follow, including one by the Deputy Governor on the Thursday.
RBA communication does seem to have changed, not just following the RBA Review but also more
recently. There may be fewer policy meetings each year, but the communication outside that certainly
feels more frequent than a couple of years ago. Back in 2024, speaking engagements felt a little

sparse outside the post-meeting media conference and parliamentary appearances. We have counted
58 public engagements so far this year on the Speeches page on the RBA’s website. This compares
with 58 for the whole of last year and 52 in 2024. Excluding the ‘compulsory’ press conferences and
parliamentary appearances, fewer than half of the 2024 events were related to economics, markets
and monetary policy.
The ‘how’ has changed
The way the RBA communicates with markets has changed as well. There are fewer set-piece

speeches on policy and a lot more semi-scripted ‘fireside chats’. Compared with a few years ago, there
are more interviews across a wide range of media. This is a big change from the pre-Review world. It
was a huge deal when the previous Governor appeared on the 7:30 Report; it is hard to imagine him
agreeing to an Assistant Governor appearing on 60 Minutes.
Part of the change reflects increased efforts to reach a general audience. Central banks want to explain

why they do what they do. This matters especially when interest rates are rising but the household
sector is not the source of the inflation.
Speaking differently to different audiences can sometimes confuse markets. As the RBA’s Chief
Communication Officer highlighted in a recent panel discussion, the post-meeting media conference is
designed to speak to the public. Market participants and economists certainly tune in, but we are not
sometimes caused confusion when tone seems to vary. Different audiences call for different tone and

varying complexity.
The content has evolved, too. RBA officials are increasingly using speeches directed to professional
audiences to discuss more technical estimates of inferred variables such as neutral rates and
financial conditions. They are also highlighting that monetary policy combats inflation in more ways
than just squeezing mortgage holders.
One thing has not changed: the RBA is not in the business of providing ‘forward guidance’ promises

about the future path of interest rates. It does, however, give more running commentary about how it
is interpreting recent data. These interpretations are themselves useful signals about what the RBA
thinks it might do.

### Page 3

the primary audience. So if you think that what the Governor said in the media conference sounded
more ‘dovish’ than what another official says in a more market-oriented forum, it is partly because she
is trying to convey that the RBA understands that its recent policy actions are making things harder for
many Australians. But this is in much the same way that a doctor might say, “This might hurt a bit.” It
does, but that will not stop the treatment.

A related issue is that it is easier to control tone in pre-drafted material such as the minutes and set-
piece speeches than in unstructured Q&A. Senior central bankers are trained to be careful with their
words in unscripted settings. If the tone differs, the minutes are still more likely to say what the Board
really means than an off-the-cuff answer.
Greater communication with the broader public might have reduced the pressure to make the set-piece

speeches more accessible. Research on central bank communication often uses measures such as
the Flesch–Kincaid Grade Level and Flesch Reading Ease score. A comprehensive analysis was
beyond the scope of this note. However, a spot comparison suggests four speeches from 2026 (1, 2, 3,
4) were harder to read than four from 2021 (1, 2, 3, 4). The 2021 speeches scored in the 11 to 12
range, which is high-school-level complexity. The 2026 speeches scored between 12 and 15, which is

academic or technically dense text requiring university-level education. (Including two of my own 2021
speeches is an obvious caveat, but the two speeches by then-Governor Phil Lowe also scored better
on these metrics than the sample from this year. It should be noted, though, that readability metrics do
not necessarily imply better clarity if the concepts discussed are more abstract.)
The ‘what’ has changed
As noted above, the content of RBA communication has evolved, too. As its intellectual framework has

begun to put more weight on the balance of supply and demand, and those invisible ‘star’ variables
(NAIRUs, neutral real rates, ‘financial conditions’, etc.), the communication to professional audiences
has increasingly focused on those topics.
More recently, the RBA has also placed more emphasis on the full range of transmission channels from
interest rates to inflation (though not, as we have noted previously, the labour supply channel). This is

understandable in a world of supply shocks. People need to understand that monetary policy is not
simply an exercise in hitting mortgage borrowers. We doubt they would be comforted, though, by the
Deputy Governor’s explanation this week that the ‘cashflow channel’ was the smallest of the
transmission channels from interest rates to inflation, with the exchange rate much more prominent.
That implies a lot of pain for a small group, for little effect on inflation. It raises the question of whether

other policy tools or institutional arrangements might get the same effect on inflation through the other
channels without hitting a minority of households disproportionately.
We have also seen a move away from using catchphrases to summarise the position. From ‘narrow
path’ to ‘not ruling anything in or out’ to ‘cautious and predictable’, once entrenched, a phrase can
outlast its usefulness.

### Page 4

Related articles
One thing that has not changed is the way the minutes record the presentations to the Board and the
members’ discussion. The minutes rarely convey much new information beyond the post-meeting
statements. Sometimes, though, the minutes reveal the Board’s reaction to the staff’s analysis. The
August 2024 minutes were a good example: the discussion highlighted just how sensitive the forecasts
were to key judgements by the staff.
The ‘why’ has not changed

Despite these changes, the underlying objectives of the RBA’s communication have not changed. It
wants to explain – and perhaps justify – its decisions. And it wants to describe how it interprets
incoming data, especially inflation, the labour market and the balance of supply and demand.
The RBA, like any central bank, is not really in the business of providing ‘forward guidance’ about
future moves. Circumstances change, so it can never guarantee a particular future path for interest

rates. Some academic literature argues that promising to keep rates low for a long time (or high, for
that matter) independently amplifies the transmission of monetary policy beyond the effect of current-
period interest rates alone, by shifting the short end of the yield curve. Central banks including the RBA
have certainly tried jawboning in this way from time to time. The prevailing view, though, is that the
potential cost of reputational loss if things do not turn out as predicted outweighs the benefits of a

slightly stronger transmission mechanism.
In this context, it is the interpretation of data that provides the signal about future policy directions. This
is why it was noteworthy that the Governor still characterised the labour market as ‘a little tight’ after the
September meeting, even though the Board’s post-meeting communication did not. We have been
noting for some time that the RBA staff have a downbeat view of supply capacity. Together with the

RBA’s oft-stated concerns about inflation, this is why the Deputy Governor’s ‘hawkishness’ did not
surprise us.

Document 401: Taiwan Earnings Call Weekly Recap - Is Earnings Still Enough to Drive Stock Price_ _ GSTW

Source PDF: all report pdf/Taiwan Earnings Call Weekly Recap - Is Earnings Still Enough to Drive Stock Price_ _ GSTW.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-30
